# Third-party notices

AgentFiles original source is licensed under MIT. Dependencies retain their own terms.

This installed-package attribution snapshot includes runtime, browser, native and development/build dependencies. It preserves the full shipped license and notice files, including bundled third-party notices. Package metadata is a label, not a substitute for these texts. It is not an assertion that every listed package is shipped in every artifact.

Generated from the public lockfile and installed packages with `node scripts/generate-notices.mjs`. Line endings and trailing whitespace are normalized; upstream notice content is otherwise preserved.

Platform snapshot: win32/x64; 268 installed package locations, 50 lockfile package locations absent and not inspected. See [dependency review](docs/dependencies.md) for scope, attribution and redistribution instructions. Regenerate and review on each distribution platform; retain any additional notices required by its actual artifacts.

## Data and native attribution

- caniuse-lite contains the Can I Use browser support dataset, authored by the Can I Use contributors, compressed by the caniuse-lite project (Ben Briggs and contributors). Sources: https://caniuse.com/ and https://github.com/browserslist/caniuse-lite. License: https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/. AgentFiles makes no modifications to this installed dataset. Its complete CC BY 4.0 text is reproduced below.
- The argon2 npm wrapper is MIT; its bundled Argon2 reference implementation is offered under CC0 1.0 or Apache 2.0. Both complete texts and the implementation's authors are preserved below. A separate embedded CC0 notice for Thomas Pornin's encoding implementation is also reproduced.
- Platform esbuild and Rollup packages without a shipped license file use the full notice from the matching installed package; this is explicitly marked below.

/*

 * software. If not, they may be obtained at the above URLs.
 */

/*
 * Example code for a decoder and encoder of "hash strings", with Argon2
 * parameters.
 *
 * This code comprises three sections:
 *
 *   -- The first section contains generic Base64 encoding and decoding
 *   functions. It is conceptually applicable to any hash function
 *   implementation that uses Base64 to encode and decode parameters,
 *   salts and outputs. It could be made into a library, provided that
 *   the relevant functions are made public (non-static) and be given
 *   reasonable names to avoid collisions with other functions.
 *
 *   -- The second section is specific to Argon2. It encodes and decodes
 *   the parameters, salts and outputs. It does not compute the hash
 *   itself.
 *
 * The code was originally written by Thomas Pornin <pornin@bolet.org>,
 * to whom comments and remarks may be sent. It is released under what
 * should amount to Public Domain or its closest equivalent; the
 * following mantra is supposed to incarnate that fact with all the
 * proper legal rituals:
 *
 * ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 * This file is provided under the terms of Creative Commons CC0 1.0
 * Public Domain Dedication. To the extent possible under law, the
 * author (Thomas Pornin) has waived all copyright and related or
 * neighboring rights to this file. This work is published from: Canada.
 * ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 * Copyright (c) 2015 Thomas Pornin
 */
